Report · page 37

Handicapper caught out out by Louise

YACHT HANDICAP 3 miles, 7 OCT 2000

Handicapper caught out out by Louise

Continual rain throughout the day resulted in a much smaller field than has been normal in the last few years but one runner who wasn't going to be put off by the conditions was 12 year old Louise Collier who sprang an almighty surprise on the handicapper. Setting off 8 minutes after Charlie Manning, she caught him at the Windmill to go into the lead and running some three minutes faster than 12 months ago, built up a huge lead by the finish. Louise's time was the fourth fastest female time that we have recorded for this course - a super effort.

Veterans dominated the men's section of the field and both Fred Riach and Don Anderson ran well to pick up the minor prizes with Veteran's Team Manager Tony Binda proving to be fastest of the day.

Report · page 37

Weston and Collier in good form

WOMEN'S SURREY C.C. LEAGUE DIVISION ONE RACE 1 - TILFORD, 14 OCT 2000

Weston and Collier in good form

Forceful running came from Rachel Weston who led home the "Belles" at Tilford. Both Tania Sturton and Syreeta Stracey backed up well and as a result the team came home 5th - considerably better than last year's initial position of 8th. Short of runners for the best of reasons - marriage and pregnancies - hopes remained high that we could emulate last winter's final position of 4th in the league.

Louise Collier was outstanding in the under 13 event. It was the shortest race of the day but her winning margin of 47 seconds was the largest in all of the races. What a shame that young Lizzy Hodges was unable to run through illness. In every race this summer past Lizzy has been just those few seconds behind Louise and the pair of them could form the basis of a really powerful team in the next few years.

Letter · page 37

Thanks Harriers!

Gordon Biscoe

In an unprecedented season, when cross country races have been prohibited or cancelled in Richmond Park; Brockwell Park and Farthing Down, due to the poor ground conditions, we were still able to host the Surrey League race on January 13 thanks to the kind offices of our friend the Ranger, Mr Jim Reader.

I would like to thank all the officials and helpers on the day, both on the Common and in Belgrave Hall, but in particular make special mention of our Course Markers.

They stood for nearly two hours in cold, wet and soggy conditions, as well as having to cope with the mindless idiots who moved a considerable quantity of our flags in the wooded area. In spite of this they still managed to ensure the races went through without trouble.

So led by our Course Marker Don Anderson, a sincere 'thank you' to Gerald Collins; John Davies; George Flanagan; Charlie Henn; Brian Pritchard; Frank Riach; Bob Taylor and Ted Weldin.

Quite simply, without guys like you, cross country races could not take place.

Gordon Biscoe ( R & CC Management Committee Secretary )

Jim ReaderGordon BiscoeDon AndersonGerald CollinsJohn DaviesGeorge FlanaganCharlie HennBrian PritchardFrank RiachBob TaylorTed Weldin

Report · page 38

Freary fights but Bels. beaten at Brockwell Park

Road & Country

SWEATSHOP SURREY C.C. LEAGUE DIVISION ONE RACE 1 - BROCKWELL PARK, 14 OCT 2000

Freary fights but Bels. beaten at Brockwell Park

Admittedly our men's team had decided that this winter our priority races were to be the Southern and National Relays and Cross Country Championships and that we would let the league take care of itself, but with a few days to go the team manager was frantically trying to round up some extra men when it appeared that we were going to be much shorter of runners than previously anticipated. As it happened we didn't fare as badly as we might have done but Thames Hare & Hounds grabbed a lead which would be very difficult to pull back.

Herne Hill's ever-present Dave Taylor, the league's individual winner last winter, soon showed his intentions by opening up a lead which stretched to nearly a minute by the finish. For the Bels, after one lap, Yacin Yusuf and Paul Freary were handily placed at the front of the chasing pack with a whole bunch of claret and gold vests around the 20s. A revitalised South London Harriers were being led by their Captain Stuart Major but it was already clear that Thames were the team in charge.

Going out again Major seemed to be getting the better of Freary and Yusuf who were battling with SLH's James and Sykes with the leading Thames men also in close order. Soon, however, an ill-looking Yacin seemed to be drifting back and half a mile from the finish paid the penalty for partaking of a pre-race tuna and sweet corn sandwich - and pulled out.

Up the penultimate hill Paul Freary gained enormously and as they turned a to drop down again the Belgrave man eased right up to Major's shoulder and seemed to biding his time before striking out for second place but 200 metres later it was the figure in the SLH vest that had broken away by 15 metres with two more of his club mates lining themselves up for an attack on our Vice-Captain.

Now up that final drag to the finish and as white vests gained on Paul, so he gained on Stuart Major - and overtook him within a few metres of the finish line.

New member Fabien Bernard-Gaudin, only 18 years old, had a terrific run to gain 13th place but as an unregistered athlete could not score for us. This young Frenchman is studying Town Planning at Westminster College and will be in London for a couple of years. Not far behind came Richard Hayman running his best ever race and at only a few days past his 21st birthday another great prospect.

Will Cockerell raised his game to a new level. Last winter he was the only man who scored in all four league races - usually coming home in the late 30s - and here he was on the verge of getting into the teens. A big effort came from Joachim Wolf, finishing within two seconds of training partner Rob McHarg, and outstanding efforts weren't restricted to the scoring ten either as Adrian Jowett ran like a man possessed to put four Belgravians behind him.

Report · page 39

Road Relay Boys win Irish Championship

Road & Country

LISBURN AC MILLENNIUM RELAYS, WALLACE PARK, LISBURN, NORTHERN IRELAND. 6 x 3.3 kms ROAD RELAY, inc. IRISH CHAMPS., 21 OCT 2000

Road Relay Boys win Irish Championship

This was the 40th running of Lisburn AC's relay and the local club's organiser Glen Grant had managed to persuade FILA and the Lisburn Borough Council to sponsor the event and turn it into something really special. Teams were invited from the Republic of Ireland, Scotland, England and Wales and the event also incorporated the Athletic Association of Ireland's Championship. The men's relay was just one event on a two day program that featured 13 races and the total prize money available was no less than £10,000. Above all the organisation by Glen and his friendly crew was superb.

When our team gathered in the hotel lounge the evening before the relay there was much deliberation regarding the running order, additionally complicated by the fact that Messrs. Lobo and Freary had turned up with colds. In the end the consensus was that we would turn out our men in order of expected increasing strength. With that matter resolved, minds then turned to the prediction of individual and team times and after the runners turned in for the night it soon became noticeable that those whose cerebral machinery had been most thoroughly lubricated by Guinness came up with the fastest clockings! All seemed pretty certain that we would win.

A superb autumn day with the bluest of skies saw 21 teams set out around Lisburn's Wallace Park. Each stage took in three circuits of a 1.1 km course which could easily be bisected by the fitter spectators. Among unfamiliar team colours Jason Lobo's claret and gold vest was immediately identified as he followed early leader Fisher of Willowfield. Clonliffe's Noel Cullen burst ahead on the second circuit but Jason, whose job was to bring us home in the top half dozen, was settled into the chasing bunch and far from a simple top six place, handed over to Rob McHarg in 2nd with a time that stood up as our fastest until the final stage.

Clonliffe now increased their lead. They had turned in two of the day's quickest times but Finn Valley also came into contention thanks to a fine run by the tough Mark Morgan who battled past our own Rob.

Yet another hot run came from Clonliffe on stage 3. Finn Valley dropped back down the field behind a rock steady Alaster Stewart, only for North Belfast to scoot past and gain 2nd, 10 seconds behind the leaders. The Bels. were now 22 seconds off the pace while Scotland's Cambuslang Harriers had moved up to 4th, just 6 seconds further adrift.

Fireworks! Throwing aside his pre-race plan to increase pace on each circuit, Paul Freary set about the men in front by clocking our two fastest lap splits to that point and then hanging on for dear life. Belgrave moved ahead for the first time but Paul's final tour of the park was painful as he followed those two 3:10 splits with a 3:27 - our slowest of the day! He'd done the damage though and with four stages gone the Bels had 4 seconds in hand over Clonliffe and 5 seconds over North Belfast - and what a finish we had to come!

A smoothly running Yacin Yusuf opened up the lead to 28 seconds on his stint and with Spencer Barden running the fastest of the day, our advantage was over one and a quarter minutes by the time Spenny raised his arms aloft at the race end. Regular winners of this event, Annandale Striders, came into the picture for the first time to get second and win the Irish Championship but the Maxwell Cup was now destined to bear a new name for the first time - Belgrave Harriers!

6-Stage Relay, 21 October. Teams: 1, Belgrave H 58:06 2, Annandale Striders 59:25 3, North Belfast AC 59:29

Fastest: 1, S.Barden (Belgrave H) 9:20 2, J.Ferrin (N.Belfast) 9:27 3, N.Cullen (Clonliffe) 9:34

Belgrave runners:

5 km races, 22 October. The top runners from the previous day's relay were all invited to take part in the Invitation 5 kms. After sizing up the opposition (mostly Belgrave) Morgan made a pretty shrewd move by opting instead for the open race and he proved too much of a handful for our own Kevin Nash who nevertheless ran a good race with a powerful finish to gain second.

The invitation event bore a striking resemblance to an old fashioned club championship with all six of our relay runners in the small line-up. The weather was by now cold, gusty and rainy as Yacin Yusuf, the smallest man in the field, took the pack off the start on the highway and into the park. Nothing changed really until the last of the four circuits when Spencer took off like a scalded cat in his dash for the cash. Yacin held up well to stay ahead of the rest as Belgrave runners took the bulk of the prizes.

Report · page 40

Six stage drama - Bels fight back from 39th to 6th

Road & Country

AAA 6 x 5.5 kms ROAD RELAY CHAMPIONSHIP SUTTON PARK, SUTTON COLDFIELD, 28 OCT 2000

Six stage drama - Bels fight back from 39th to 6th

On a day when Sutton Park was blasted with tree splitting winds and driving rain we overcame a disastrous start to get up to 6th - one minute and one second away from bronze - and hang on to our unofficial title of leading Southern Club - just! Drifts of sodden autumn leaves were plastered to the roadway to make the footing treacherous and in these conditions, with winds gusting to 60 mph and the linked metal railings along the course getting bowled over, individual times meant very little when compared to those of earlier years. Stages two and three probably had to face the worst of it but generally it was felt that the circumstances added around 30 seconds to what would normally be expected. The Belgrave FILA tent made its debut and what a good job it did even if, during the post race tea and cake ritual, the whole team had to hold on to the roof to stop the claret and gold pavilion taking off and heading for Lichfield.

With just 30 minutes left before the start and the team manager taking initial steps in a team reshuffle, our two first leg runners arrived, having been held up on the motorway due to the conditions. Jason Lobo had been suffering from a cold the previous week in Ireland but he had run so well there on the opening leg that we felt sure he would give us the start we needed. Poor old Jason! Everything conspired to hit him at once - the late arrival, the head cold, the previous weekend's two races ... After staying in the bunch for the first half of stage one where he was only 6 seconds off the lead, he then found himself running into big trouble and had to suffer the indignity of streams of runners overtaking him as he grimly made his way to the finish. "If it hadn't been a relay I would have dropped out," was his comment after he'd sent Charles Herrington on his way. An opening 39th place was not what we were looking for in our bid to get near the medals.

Charlie is working back to full fitness after the stress fracture that kept him out of action for half of the summer. Not only did he pull back seven teams and duck under 18 minutes - no mean achievement on this day - he also moved up on a whole posse of runners ahead of him to give Lee Hurst the task of picking them off. Back after a break from running, Lee duly obliged and although not happy with his time, a final position at least near to the top ten began to look possible.

One man who is in much better shape than the same time last year is Spencer Barden. Taking things steadily up the hill he then used the wind to its full advantage and blasted through the field before taking another "easy" spell and then turning it on again for the finish. Result: equal 6th fastest of the day and a time more than ten seconds faster than 1999. The rest of our guys who ran last year were between 30 and 60 seconds slower! It was a perfect run for Spenny. We had planned that he might pull us into 3rd spot at this point - and had all other things been equal he may well have done it.

Yacin Yusuf followed up by taking another five places and for the first time we went into the top ten and overtook our local rivals Thames Hare & Hounds right on the line with one stage remaining. How the slightly built Yacin managed to keep going in the gale is a marvel. It was all up to Alaster Stewart now and he didn't disappoint with one of his best ever runs around the circuit. Two more teams fell to his stride and with a thumbs up sign as he passed our supporters at the tent in the finishing straight he, and we, were delighted with our final position.

So, time to reflect: the autumn road relays were over and we had progressed well. Second best in Surrey, 4th best in the Southern with the Surrey victors behind us, 6th best in the country with every one of the Southern clubs behind us.

Report · page 40

Back to the engravers!

REDHILL & SURREY BEAGLES AC's 6 x 2.5 miles C.C. RELAY PRIORY PARK, REIGATE, 4 NOV 2000

Back to the engravers!

A sunny day in Reigate's Priory Park was a welcome change from the recent stormy weather. Three full Belgrave teams were in action and the Belles set the ball rolling in the first race where Tania Sturton clocked the fastest Belgrave time on the initial stage. Her 9th place was maintained by Debbie Hearn and Syreeta Stracey to the finish.

As last year's champions our men had returned the winners' shield, freshly engraved, but were not too certain about being able to take it home again, especially when three of the A squad became unavailable at the last minute as a result of niggling injuries, etc. Kevin Quinn was desperate to run but knee pains meant that there was a strong possibility of him having to "shut off" power during the race - so down to the Bs he went. Our A squad contained not one of the six who ran so well in the previous week's National Road Relay and, moreover, only one man from the previous year's winning Priory team.

South London's Robbie James took the initiative on stage one and built up a huge lead over Brighton & Hove who were being chased by two Belgrave men who were racing neck and neck to get into the top three. Eighteen year-old Frenchman Fabien Bernard-Gaudin, still without first team eligibility, led around the park and into the straight but Joachim Wolf had no intention of leaving things that way and closed him down to squeeze the A team ahead by inches at the takeover.

SLH's challenge faded on stage two as the red vested Brighton men took control. Our own Najibe Hliouat, running more consistently and getting better very time out, worked hard and moved into second but the order changed again on stage three when Boxhill's Paul Hayward not surprisingly got the better of Cap'n Roger, just back from honeymooning in Australia.

Brighton were 28 seconds ahead of the Bels. going into the second half of the race and with 'super vet' Nigel Gates their next man, no doubt were hoping to increase that margin. But Kevin Nash, our sole survivor from the team of '99 had other ideas. It had been another week of 100 miles for 'Gnasher' but it didn't seem to slow him any and while Gates pulled Brighton further away from the main body of the field our boy closed in on him until the gap was just 13 seconds with two laps remaining.

With the next team, Boxhill, now nearly two minutes behind, Will "Billy the Kid" Cockerell showed great confidence by moving up and forging a small lead. Back came the Brighton man during the desperate descent down that treacherous gully but Will opened up again and stormed the final part of the lap to give Andy Brown a 10 second advantage.

Now, with a raft of 1:50-1:51 800s under his belt, no-one doubted Andy's ability to deliver a winning kick should the situation arise but we were rather hoping that it wouldn't get that close. In fact it didn't. Two and a half miles is somewhat further than our man is used to racing but he was able to increase our lead by a further 8 seconds by the finish. Dave Taylor obliterated the day's previous best times with a charge that took Herne Hill from 5th to 3rd but on this occasion we were far enough away to avoid even feeling the draught.

Back to the engravers again!

Men's results

34 teams started; 30 teams finished.

Women's results

25 teams started; 23 teams finished.

Report · page 41

SOUTH OF THAMES "JUNIOR" 5 miles C. C.

LLOYD PARK, 11 NOV 2000.

On a day of cold rain with the wind battering the runners and turning the spectators' umbrellas inside out we were not sure that we'd be able to turn out four men to make up a team but not only did we achieve that, we ended up not so far off the medals. Policeman Rob Harding turned up in spite of being "on nights" and Jerome Curtat, now living in Paris but on a flying visit to London to see his brother, had thoughtfully brought his kit with him.

South London's Stuart Major was in a class apart and took an early lead which rapidly grew until he was lost to sight by the main field. Our own Joachim Wolf led the Belgrave line-up and after a mile or so he was placing 4th with Rob leading the rest of the team in the early 20s. With the exception of Warren Lynch, however, the conditions took their toll on our lads over the second circuit with Joachim encountering stomach problems and Rob and Jerome drifting back somewhat. Long distance man Warren seemed to revel in the circumstances. The further the race went, the stronger he looked. Another couple of laps would have suited him fine and we relished the thought of seeing how he would tackle 9 miles of Parliament Hill mud in January!

7, J.Wolf 31:04; 15, W.Lynch 32:50; 25, R.Harding 33:43; 36, J.Curtat 34:37; 92, H.Corbett 38:09; 133 finished.

Report · page 41

Thames Hare & Hounds pegged back in the Park

SWEATSHOP SURREY C.C. LEAGUE DIVISION ONE RACE 2 - RICHMOND PARK, 18 NOV 2000

Thames Hare & Hounds pegged back in the Park

Richmond Park was at its wettest with the Ranelagh Harriers' regular course having to be adapted to avoid some of the flooded stretches. As the rain came on again it was heartening to see a good bunch of young runners set out around Sidmouth Wood and Queen Elizabeth's Plantation. Team Managers Kim and Paul Collier had gathered a full team of young runners together for the Under 17/Under 15 race (all our lads being under 15) and had two more for the Under 13s. Their efforts were well rewarded with two 2nd places in the team races and young Jamie Kirton leading the team home. With half the league now gone Belgrave now lie 2nd in the oldest age group and 3rd in the youngest.

The seniors had the huge task of trying to pull Thames Hare & Hounds back after their devastating win at Brockwell Park. There were plenty of men on hand to do their best with no fewer than 27 names listed by the time the sodden declaration sheet was handed over.

Lee Hurst was among the early leaders as the field set off in the direction of White Lodge but on the return from the loop four men had broken away - Gary Staines (SLH) and Sam Haughian (Hounslow) accompanied by our own Yacin Yusuf and Yitbarak Dinku. Yacin had turned out after changing his plans to run in the European qualifying race the next day at Margate while Yitbarak was having his first race in 10 months: we were glad to see both of them! Non-scoring Richard Ashe was close behind while Lee and Paul Freary were running together with 18 year-old Richard Ward, running his first Surrey League race in claret & gold, keeping an eye on them.

Setting out again, the positions were pretty much unchanged. A whole posse of Thames men came through together in the 'teens but with five men in the 20s to back up our high-placers a win looked safely on the cards. Richard Hayman was not looking too comfortable but those "toughies" Rob McHarg, Will Cockerell and Joachim Wolf were running strongly with Norwegian Knut Hegvold also making an impression.

At the finish Hounslow's Sam Haughian was an easy winner but the broadest grin came from Yacin who kept a one second margin between himself and our life-member Gary Staines. Yitbarak was so impressive in his first race of the season and he has every intention of improving on his 4th place in the races to come. As normal a blistering sprint gained Will Cockerell an extra place just inches before the finishing funnel was reached and another to run way above expectations was Phil Carstairs who had great pleasure in pushing back the "Skipper", not having a happy run, to 10th scoring position.

Ten men home in 39; a pleasing win and 64 points pulled back on our rivals. Another one like that after Christmas and the final race would promise to be exciting. Game on!

Great Win from Under 13 "Belles"

WOMEN'S SURREY C.C. LEAGUE DIVISION ONE RACE 2 - LIGHTWATER, 2 DEC 2000

Great Win from Under 13 "Belles"

Louise Collier again led home the field in the League's youngest age group - the under 13s. Her winning margin of 23 seconds over this shorter than normal course meant that for much of the race she was out of sight of the chasing bunch. Unlike the race at Tilford, however, this time Louise had team mates highly placed to back her up and with Jessie Massey in second place and Lizzy Hodges in fourth, the team scored a great win over Guildford & Godalming. As a squad they moved up into 3rd place overall behind Sutton & District and Dorking & Mole Valley.

The seniors didn't have quite such a happy time with rivals Tania Sturton and Syreeta Stracey being the only two Belgrave runners to squeeze into the top 30. With only 10th team place on the day they slipped back down the league to 7th and now have a battle on their hands over the last two matches to ensure that they keep clear of the relegation zone.

Report · page 42

It's low-key at Lloyd Park

SURREY COUNTY CROSS COUNTRY CHAMPIONSHIPS LLOYD PARK, 6 JAN 2001

It's low-key at Lloyd Park

Again we were unable to produce a strong presence for the County Championships. Hopes were high for a good placing in the youngest girls' age group with Louise Collier having been in fine form in recent league races. On this occasion she was not at her best and when shaken by a severe slip in the mud, came out of the race altogether.

There were also high hopes that Richard Ward would be able to duplicate his win of last year in the junior men's race but the redoubtable talent of Reigate's Ed Prickett prevailed. Richard will now be switching his attentions to indoor track races before returning for the road relays. Running in the combined junior and under 17 women's race Debbie Hearn placed 16th overall but only just missed the medals, being fourth junior to the line.

Our women's team picked up bronze medals last year but from that squad only Tania Sturton was back on the course this time and filled exactly the same place - 25th. Up ahead new member Erica Fogg, yet another Dave Lucas trained athlete, showed that she is going to be a great asset to the team - when it gets together again - by running home in 10th place.

Shortly before the senior men's race it had looked as if Belgrave were not going to be able to start a team with only four runners and spectating Don Anderson on the course with 10 minutes left before the pistol was due to be fired. Then Lloyd Catley arrived for his first ever run in Belgrave colours and Don was coerced into his kit to complete the six. Just as the runners were lining up Malachi Byansi arrived and with no warm up at all, joined the field to give us seven on the start line.

Herne Hill's Dave Taylor continues to dominate cross country events in Surrey but in this Championship he did not have it completely his own way. In muddy conditions Taylor, Belgrave's Kevin Nash and South London's Stuart Major broke away early with Staines of South London not far behind. Kevin was totally focussed on the race and before long, as Major dropped back, it was the Belgrave Harrier who tended to be forcing the pace as mud and water flew up from the remaining pair's flying feet.

Unfortunately a slight niggle in Kevin's lower back, which had caused him to cut short a training session during the week, flared up and on the second of the three circuits Taylor prised open a gap which grew to about 20 seconds. Both runners were still going away from the rest of the field where all sorts of drama was taking place. Major fell; Staines and Herne Hill's second scorer Boyle came through; Staines then had a stomach problem and dropped back alarmingly before finally making sure of a bronze medal at the very end of the race.

Our own Roger Alsop, short of training as a result of various injuries in recent months, started steadily and progressed through the field for a satisfactory 21st at the end. Malachi and Lloyd were within a few places of each other for most of the race. Both are gradually gaining fitness but at the end it was Mal who proved the strongest with Lloyd drifting back until he found himself under attack from Erwann Lemenager who was having a great run. It was good to see Steve Zealy back in action for the Bels. again - the 13 years since he formed part of our National team seem to have flown!

Report · page 42

Bels. hammered at home!

SURREY CROSS COUNTRY LEAGUE DIV ONE RACE 3 - WIMBLEDON COMMON, 13 JAN 2001

Bels. hammered at home!

With a huge absentee list we were always going to be up against the task of keeping close to, or even stealing a few more points away from, Thames Hare & Hounds in our quest to retain the Surrey League title. Four top Belgrave runners were taking the chance of overseas or international standard competition, work commitments pulled away another four, injury accounted for two and communication breakdown took out a couple more. Still we lined up twenty-three men; and didn't they battle magnificently to get us into 2nd place on the day. Frankly though, Thames were in great form and took full advantage of our weakened state.

Our second year of running on the new course revealed a few delights that we hadn't expected: drifts of dried leaves in the woods hid a black porridge of the most glutinous variety. Even the team manager got a taste of this as, at the end of the day, he found himself the guardian of a pair of the most horrendously slimed Reeboks left behind by a Belgrave runner who couldn't bear to put them in his kitbag.

Jason Lobo led the team home with Rob McHarg continuing his patch of good form (3rd in the Berkshire Champs. the previous week). Twenty-one year-old Richard Hayman made another advance up the order to place 16th and a betting man might well be advised to have a few bob on Rick's chances of spending next year's series in the top ten. The middle order troops did us proud, running way above expectations, and especially welcome were new men Lloyd Catley and Guy Lavender - first Surrey League runs from them and both in the "ten". As "Our Will" was heard to say whilst savouring his curry, "We might not have won and the league may be slipping away but it was one of the best ever efforts from a Belgrave team." With a points score identical to that of Herne Hill Harriers, second team position was decided on the position of each club's 10th man home - and 52 year-old Charlie Dickinson swung it for us.

So, is it out of sight? We'd have to say that chances of lifting the title for 2000-2001 look very slim!

Very many thanks to all those who marshalled, marked the course, judged and timed the result and helped with results. Particularly well done to Don Anderson: having laid out the trail he then had to remark it after someone attempted to sabotage the event by neatly removing every flag and trace of tape over a whole section of the route. Thank goodness for mobile 'phones enabling the sending of a warning to hold back the senior race while the track was remarked.

How nice to see a Belgrave vest leading home the Young Athletes' field. James Skilbeck Nelson had a good lead over the next man and with a full complement of Youths and Boys we took the team race by a convincing margin. Paul Graham, second Belgrave lad home, also impressed. He was lying second when going off course due to the missing flags and by the time he was back on the route had lost two places. Over the last 12 months he has become a representative of Suffolk County at Walking, Track running and Cross-country. Brother Andrew, also a race-walker, headed our Colts' team.

Column · page 43

In tough and freezing conditions Paul Freary won the Ribble Valley 10 kms at Clitheroe on New Year's Eve in 31:07. It was a close run thing and Paul had to use all of his experience to beat the young Ethiopian Getu Assefa running for Pendle.

Paul Evans spent New Year's Day chasing Kenyans in the Corrida de Houilles 1 kms in Paris. He was pleased with his 29:31 for 6th place behind Sammy Kipketer's winning 28:31.

Nearer home, in Hyde Park to be precise, Belgrave clocked up their first team win of the year in the Serpentine Runners 10 kms. Al Stewart 4th in 32:28 was followed by Mal Byansi 9th 34:10, Paul Smoothy 17th 35:42 and Will Cockerell 20th 36:16.

Toby Tanser won over 5 miles in New York on January 7th, 25:19, and a week later was first home again in a 10 kms races with 31:59.

Medical Man Charles Herrington was called in to work to cover an emergency on the day of our home Surrey League match but the following day he turned out in the Fred Hughes Memorial 10 miles at St. Albans. After a mile he found himself easily going away from the field and a steady run in 53:12 gave him victory by 3 minutes.

Birhan Dagne ran her first race as a Belgravian on 14 January. Competing in the Hastings 10k she ran 36:32 for 4th behind Kenyan Sally Barsosio's winning 33:39.

Yet another successful trip to the sun for Paul Freary. In the Bermuda International 10 kms on 13 January our running Vice-Captain clocked 31:50 to place 7th but he was obviously saving a little bit because the following day he was back on the tarmac again to win the Bermuda International Half-Marathon in 69:36. It was his 5th win in the event - a terrific record.

Hot form was shown by Belgrave runners in the 4 kms Reebok Cross Challenge at Cardiff on Sunday 14 January. Mark Miles won in 13:27 from clubmate Spencer Barden, 13:29. Tonbridge's Andrew Graffin was 3rd, 13:37, and Christian Stephenson of Cardiff was 4th, 13:46. Both Belgravians look to be in the sort of shape that should see them earn selection for the World Cross Country Championship 4 kms event.

A super run came from Guy Lavender in a county championship that we won't mention by name. Suffice it to say that he competed under another athlete's name just to get the run in - and found himself in the embarassing position of nearing the finish in the top ten and thereby running into contention for a Inter-Counties selection. Obviously that would have blown his cover so one and a half minutes were spent virtually jogging on the spot until he was safely back in the pack!

Alaster Stewart was training hard in January in Hong Kong and looking forward to returning to the UK for the National Cross Country Championship and the following road relays. He won a 13km race held mostly over rough paths and made the South China Morning Post, Hong Kong's national newspaper: "Stuart (sic) who runs for top ranked Belgrave Harriers in the UK . . " Al clocked 41:05 in spite of the testing conditions.

Spencer Barden was second Briton home in the FILA/Belfast 8 kms on January 20th. He was 9th in 24:52 to Kenyan Gachara's winning 24:18. Paul Evans placed 14th in 25:07.

New member Juliette Clark placed 4th in the Mitcham 25 kms event on January 21st, clocking 1:44:07 for 4th.

WOMEN'S SURREY C.C. LEAGUE DIVISION ONE RACE 3 - LIGHTWATER PARK, BAGSHOT, 13 JAN 2001

It was back to Lightwater for match three in the series. The race was scheduled to have been held in Richmond Park but the authorities deemed that with so much wet weather recently, the Park was likely to be damaged by the feet of so many harriers.

It was a very sparse turnout from our seniors who placed last on the day and now seem destined for division two again. After three matches the Belles lie in 9th place, although a good turn-out for the final race could save the day.

Things looked brighter in the younger age-groups, however, and both the under 13 and under 15 teams finished up in second place with Jessie Massey and Lizzie Hodges filling the two runners up slots in the younger race.

Report · page 43

SOUTH OF THAMES C.C. CHAMPIONSHIP ROEHAMPTON, 13 JAN 2001

After three consecutive team wins in this championship, and in spite of the fact that the race was held on (almost) home turf, the magnificent Dewar Shield found new keepers for the next twelve months.

It was a difficult situation. It would have been great to have extended our winning streak, and we certainly had enough men left who had not been barred from the event, but in the middle of a five week run of races something had to go - and this was it.

Steve Zealy was our only legitimate runner, in 62nd spot, but right up the front second-claimer Richard Ashe carried our colours to an outstanding win - although as a guest his name did not feature on the SoT result sheet. Fabien Bernard-Gaudin, still awaiting clearance to score for us, suffered a similar fate.

Report · page 43

A chance missed ...

SOUTH of ENGLAND AA CROSS COUNTRY CHAMPIONSHIPS PARLIAMENT HILL, 27 JAN 2001

A chance missed ...

So much for targets. For our men's team this event was to have been the first of four events that we were to concentrate on. Our league campaign and winning streak in the South of Thames were forfeited for - a team position three places lower than last year.

Paul Freary, who lives 10 minutes from where the Northern Championships were being held, drove 200 miles each way to hit the Hill hard to go over the top in second place. He'd not been going as well as he'd have liked in training recently but after struggling on the first lap, after that powerful start, fought his way back and was satisfied with a place in the top 20.

It was another great run from Jason Lobo, who lives even closer to the Northern venue than Paul but travelled south to put himself to the test over that famous Parliament Hill mud. It was a morass, so thick in places that runners could hardly pull their feet out of it and yet here we had a 1:45 800m man coming home in 19th spot and ribbing Paul that he might have had a go at him if he'd not thought that it might upset him to see a half-miler come sailing past after 15 kilometres. Every one of the rest of the guys ran well, particularly Phil Carstairs who placed a best ever 62nd and would have been higher still but for a fall.

Teamwise, it was a chance missed. Thames Hare & Hounds were awesome with 6 in 44 - and they won the South of Thames the previous week and will almost certainly take the league as well.

Erica Fogg backed up her Surrey County run with another fine effort and with Tania Sturton and Syreeta Stracey in action we weren't so far away from a full team. Fabien Bernard-Gaudin had to pull out of the Junior Men's race; he broke a toe before Christmas and his recovery was not as complete as he had thought.

Feature · page 44

Just a coincidence

Mavis Hall took part in her first walking race at the John Hedgethorne Memorial event at Chelmsford on 28th January. A very well supported event saw Mavis coming home in 141st place over the three mile course. By coincidence husband, Eric, managed the same position in his first ever race, the Belgrave Open back in 1948. Mavis finished strongly attempting to catch her 9 year old grand daughter Brittany who was also entered for the Club. Unfortunately Brittany was unable to catch Granddad although she finished just ahead of the 1966 Commonwealth Games gold medallist Ron Wallwork whc, we understand, was suffering from the handicap of pushing his grandson round in a pushchair. Intrepid characters these walkers.

Mavis HallMavisEricBrittanyRon Wallwork

Report · page 44

Captain Barclay's Millennium Walk & RWA Champs.

EWH

12-13 August 2000

While many Club members were performing to such high standards in Birmingham our long distance walker, Paul King, was taking part in the Captain Barclay Millennium walk at the Newmarket race course. The competitors in Birmingham competed over the weekend but for Paul it was a matter of walking for a whole twenty four hours in an attempt to cover 100 miles.

Starting at 3 pm on the very hot Saturday he set off with the sixty plus competitors from Tattersalls, along the High Street and across the Heath more used to the sight of multi million pound thoroughbreds showing their paces! After this fairly high profile start the rest of the event took place around what is known in racing circles as the Rowley Mile and the new Grandstand complex. The weekend was very hot and although part of the course was favoured with a pleasant breeze there was little shelter. Paul maintained his steady pace throughout the night and despite a bad patch in the early morning he responded to the encouragement of the small band of spectators and officials. Also offering assistance as dawn broke were fellow Belgravians Carl Lawton, George Beecham and Ray Middleton. Paul stuck to the task and gradually got back into his stride managing to achieve the target with some time to spare and thus join that select band of Belgravians who have qualified to call themselves Centurions.

Paul survived conditions which saw the retirement of many including such men as Don Thompson, the Rome Olympic Gold Medallist and others of similar experience.

Well done Paul!

Some, of course, such as George Beecham, seem to revel in the heat and George's first outing on the Sunday morning was limited by the organisers to 3 miles so once that was out of the way George opted to take part in a fun walk of around 5 miles on the famous turf itself! We are not sure but it is possible that he was tempted by the fact that many of the other participants were female clad suitably for the high temperature of the day - Ray and Jean Middleton also took part but Percy Wright opted to sit this one out!

EWH

Paul KingPaulCarl LawtonGeorge BeechamGeorgeRay MiddletonRayJean MiddletonDon ThompsonPercy WrightEWH

Learn about electric timing and photo finish

We have some top class electrical timing and judging equipment at Battersea Park but we need to build a team to enable us to use it on a regular basis. We will be setting it up and running familiarisation courses before the track season commences - probably late February or March. If you have any of track judging, timekeeping, and basic computer skills (used to using keyboard, mouse, windows software) or have some knowledge of cabling up computers or other hi-tech equipment and you fancy getting involved, please call Alan on [phone number withheld]. Even if you have none of these but would like to learn then we want to hear from you.

Feature · page 45

The Runningman

www.runningman.com

Remember the Running Man? In our last issue we reported that he had run across four continents and twenty-five countries and we left him in Central America. Now, eight months later, he's running across the USA, heading in the general direction of Kansas City, Missouri.

En route to the USA he was held at gunpoint in Panama City, about 10 km North of The Panama Canal. The two young criminals hesitated because of passing drivers, so he used that opportunity to break free. Another attempt to rob him occurred in Acapulco, where three guys tried to cut him off and rob him at gunpoint. But: "I finally made it out of the third world after what had been years, crossing into The United States. What a relief and a shock. Everything looked organised, clean and new and I couldn't get over people speaking in English."

On Saturday 7 December 1996, Briton Robert Garside, then 28 years old and an ex-psychology student, set out from Piccadilly Circus, London. Four years later and approaching his mid-thirties, having run across Europe, Africa, Asia, Australasia and South America, he is taking on North America before the hardest part of his odyssey - Antarctica . He spent Christmas in Denver, Colorado where he found it pretty cold but then as he says, "It's probably because it was the first winter I have been in since Tibet". So how is he going to find running across the frozen wastes of the south?

" I think of it as the final frontier because it will be my final continent on this mammoth world run. Also because in terms of it being a challenge, it surely has to be the most difficult, tougher than The Himalayas and after crossing all the other continents, it will be the last big running challenge."

But already Robert is planning ahead. In 2005, The Runningman will become The Swimmingman and attempt 'the first swim around the world', beginning from the coast of Athens, Greece. He's giving himself five years to complete this effort, which he has named Mission Two. And then, in 2015, comes Mission Three when The Runningman will become The Flyingman and attempt the first flight around the world, without the use of engines or balloons. (How? Will he be using a one man self launching glider?) Only then will his two-decade conquest of land, sea and air be complete; a combined total distance of that to the moon, with all three voyages solo and world record-breaking firsts!

Robert GarsideRobert

Women: 93, S.Couzens 73:02; 160, P.Lively 84:34. 16 Reebok Cross Challenge, Cumbernald 8 kms: 4, S.Barden 26:12; 10, M.Miles 26:40 17 Ronhill-LARA Surrey Classic 10 kms, Epsom: Having already won the previous Ronhill-LARA Classic Surrey 10 kms, eighteen year-old Richard Ward turned in another sparkling performance over the distance at Epsom when he improved his time to 30:21. This time moved him up to 4th on the Club Rankings for the year but was only good enough for second on the day - although it was no disgrace to finish within 7 seconds of international Rod Finch of Southampton Runners. Richard's performance also gave him the scalp of local rival Gary Staines who placed 3rd in 30:30. 2, R.Ward 30:21; 10, M.Webb 34:01; 19, A.Binda 35:02. 25 Lauriston Christmas Paalauf: 7, G.Adams/A.Grimes (Herc.Wimb) 5m 872y; 10, C.Dickinson/J.Thomas (Herc.Wimb.) 5m 778y. 26 Bedford 10 miles: In a rough race where little seasonal good will was shown, several runners were brought down but Phil Carstairs survived to place 5th. 5, P.Carstairs 56:34. 30 BUPA Great North CC, Durham. Belgravians featured in virtually the last races of the year at Durham where Spencer Barden made much of the running in the 4 km event, breaking away from a classy field, only to be beaten by Ireland's Turnbull on the run in to the tape. Mark Miles had travelled up in the hope of getting into that short race but found it full and had to switch to the 9 kms event. Kenya's Kosgei had the measure of the international field but, in spite of running a little too easily to start with and letting the some of the top contenders get away, Mark had a great run to place 12th. He was third Englishman home. Charles Herrington had a grim time in the open race, running across the frozen wastes in flat shoes. He had been told minutes before the event that it was to be held on the road! 4kms: 1, G.Turnbull 11:12; 2, S.Barden 11:22; 13, J.Lobo 12:04. 9kms: 1, P.Kosgei (Kenya) 27:52; 12, M.Miles 29:15. 3.5 miles: 14, C.Herrington 19:15.
